# Preliminary Hearing, Day 2 — Transcript (July 7, 2026)
Continues the five-day preliminary hearing that began with [[2026-07-06 - Preliminary Hearing Day 1 Transcript|Day 1]] · *State of Utah v. Tyler James Robinson*, Case No. 251403576 · video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=y6ofpz6ReuE

> 🎥 **Watch the source recording** — the timestamps below deep-link into this video.

![](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=y6ofpz6ReuE)
## Summary
**What happened:** Day 2 opened with the parties agreeing to a standing hearsay objection (so [[The Defense Team|the defense]] need not re-object to every hearsay item) and resumed the direct examination of State case agent [[David Hull|Agent David Hull]], who was then cross-examined by Kathy Nester and released. After argument, [[Judge Tony Graf]] only *provisionally* admitted State's Exhibit 5.1 — a written statement from a Turning Point USA figure about the group's mission and "Orthodox Christian views" offered on the victim-targeting enhancement — declining to publish it and reserving whether it is fully relevant to [[Tyler Robinson|Robinson]]'s state of mind. In the afternoon the State called Utah DPS Sergeant Jennifer Faumuina for a limited chain-of-custody/DNA-collection purpose and then paused her, allowing [[The Defense Team|the defense]] to call its own witness out of order: FBI DNA forensic examiner **Amanda Bakker** (Quantico lab), whose lengthy, technical testimony probed the reliability and limits of forensic DNA comparison. The day ended with Bakker excused and court adjourned to Day 3; this remains a probable-cause hearing, and no bind-over was argued or decided.
**For the prosecution:** Established chain of custody for the rooftop screwdriver and the towel-wrapped rifle recovered near campus, and got its DNA case in through Faumuina's Exhibit 31 and cross of Bakker, who conceded her accredited lab followed validated, ground-truth-tested protocols in reaching its conclusions.
**For the defense:** Scored on cross of Agent Hull (investigation timeline, public tips, the fading "gait/limp," and Robinson's peaceful surrender) and used its own FBI examiner to press [[The Casing Match and DNA|DNA-reliability]] limits — that STR testing measures allele length, not sequence, and that examiner "experience" cannot by itself establish scientific validity — while keeping the [[Insufficiency of the Evidence|probable-cause]] and [[Chain of Custody Problems|custody]] questions alive.

> Speaker labels are from automated transcription plus review; verify against the video before quoting a specific person by name.

**[06:31:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=y6ofpz6ReuE&t=23481s) WITNESS — Amanda Bakker:** Sure. So first what I do is I count how many labels that I see. So how many of those little squares under each of those locations. So for example at D2S441, there are three squares. Knowing that one piece of information comes from mom and one comes from dad if it's a single source profile from one individual I expect to see no more than two squares because I see three that gives me an indication that there's at least two individuals. Same thing for the next D19S433 and as well as FGA, there were three peaks that were called. So I knew this had to be at least two individuals. Then what I do is I take a look at the peaks that are not called and determine could there potentially be a third person included. So for example at D2, the location farthest to the left, I'm asking myself that 12 in that bottom box, is it the same contributor as for example the 22 under FGA all the way to the right? Because I see peaks that are not labeled it could be that 12 belongs to one individual and those two peaks that are not called belong to a second or third individual and that at FGA I could be seeing the 22 from that third person but that second person has dropped out. Because I'm able to use information below the threshold and I see peaks, that gives me an indication that I can increase my number of contributors to three, which I did in this case.
